## Mexico Retail

Trends in Mexico Retail have been the most stable among major LatAm markets, and into 2024 we see a continued favorable setup, supported by factors such as remittance flows and lower interest rates. As detailed in Debate #1: Will the LatAm Consumer Pick Up or Muddle Through in '24? , the Morgan Stanley LatAm economics team believes Mexico GDP growth should remain resilient, with tight labor markets, strong remittances, and rising government transfers driving consumption. They look for a pickup in remittances growth and foresee real GDP growth at 3.4% in 2023 and then at 2.3% in 2024 amid a US soft landing, most likely impacting Mexico in 2H24. Our consumer survey results provide additional support for this call, with a wide and rising spread between the proportion of Mexico consumers who expect their household financial situations to improve over the next six months (72%) versus worsen (9%); see Exhibit 117 . For Mexico retail sales, we have seen +9% y/y growth on average in 11M23 ( Exhibit 118 ). While this marked a deceleration from +13% in 2022 and +16% in 2021, there was less of a growth step-change than in Brazil. Looking into 2024, top-down and category-level trends support our conviction around a continued outlook for growth.

Exhibit 117:Consumers in Mexico reported a high and rising spread between "better" and "worse" next 6 month expectations for their household financial situations

![](images/a0db4eb3098f92114f51632868bf128d6ff76f0c7e3bda6efc35458ecb959fb0.jpg)
Source: AlphaWise, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 118:Mexico ANTAD retail sales have grown +9% y/y on average in 11M23, with all major categories in positive territory

![](images/1c6601bb6d25fd1733d7566374f083557f7e7239979d3c9dbc74285e8bf6b1a8.jpg)
Source: ANTAD, Morgan Stanley Research

## Chile & Other LatAm Retail

Outside of Brazil and Mexico, we highlight a persistent overhang from lapping stimulus in Chile, and accelerating inflation but a lower official/unofficial FX gap in Argentina. In Chile, real retail sales have reversed the outsized growth trends seen in '21, with sales declining y/y for each month since May 2022 upon lapping economic stimulus from successive pension fund withdrawal rounds. Chile real retail sales growth tracked at -9% on average in 10M23, with only a modest improvement to -6% in Sept/Oct ( Exhibit 119 ). For '24, our LatAm economics team expects real GDP growth to return positive at +1.9% (-0.1% for '23), with a modest recovery on the back of still-tight policies. We see lower interest rates as a tailwind for retail sales, but believe the difficult stimulus-driven comparison overhang is likely to remain a weight. In Colombia, real retail sales have declined -4% y/y on average through 10M23 ( Exhibit 120 ); as we head into 2024, our economists remain cautious, with a slow disinflation pace and fiscal uncertainty weighing on the outlook. In Argentina, under the new Milei administration there was a December 2023 peso devaluation, which lowered the gap between the official and unofficial FX rates ( Exhibit 121 ). While in the near term, our economics team expects the FX adjustment to drive a further pickup in inflation, which accelerated throughout 2023 ( Exhibit 122 ). For detail on Argentina policy, see Argentina: A Good Start (20 Dec 2023). For LatAm Retail impacts, see Debates for 2H23: How To Treat Argentina Exposure? (7 Jun 2023).

# Interest Rates, Taxes, and Leverage

We see LatAm Retail as an interest rate exposed sector, with greater financial expense burdens in recent years amid higher benchmark rates, pandemic period debt issuance, and select acquisitions. After reaching trough levels in late-2020 / early-2021, benchmark interest rates rose to 13.75% in Brazil (from lows of 2.0%). While three 2H23 cuts brought the benchmark to 12.25%, we continue to see financial expense pressure across our coverage amid still-elevated rates. In addition to this effect, at the company level we have seen factors driving higher indebtedness — including debt issuance during peak pandemic periods, and company-specific actions such as Carrefour Brasil's Group BIG acquisition and Assai's Extra stores purchase. Coupling these factors, financial expenses as a percentage of revenue for our covered companies have increased in recent years. Financial expenses (pre-IFRS16) were 3.5% of revenue on average in 2022, up +130bps y/y and +140bps above 2020 levels; we see this figure deteriorating further to 4.2% of revenue for 2023E, though with some expected relief in 2024. As shown in Exhibit 123 , eCommerce / omni-channel operators lead for financial expense intensity, while at the country level ( Exhibit 124 ) we see a greater burden in Brazil versus Chile and Mexico.

Exhibit 123:Amid a period of rising interest rates, financial expenses have moved higher across sub-sectors, led by eCommerce / omni-channel operators…

![](images/d6688328c10b3305db85fe33164edba40c806c7ead0d9cb08a7007120b0a3664.jpg)
Note: eCommerce includes MELI, MGLU, BHIA, CASH; Apparel and DS includes LREN, CEAB, LIVEPOL, FALABELLA; Food Retail includes CRFB, ASAI, PCAR, WALMEX, CENCOSUD; Specialty Retail includes NTCO, PETZ, MBLY.
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Exhibit 124:... and at the country level, we see Brazil-based companies with the highest financial expenses as a % of revenue in '22 and '23E
![](images/ad151faf7d66f5e479821670d61ac96343885bf11054a58fa6cab0b94c545e0e.jpg)
Note: Brazil includes MGLU, BHIA, CASH, LREN, CEAB, CRFB, ASAI, PCAR, NTCO, PETZ, MBLY; Chil includes FALABELLA and CENCOSUD; Mexico includes WALMEX and LIVEPOL. Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Coupling company-specific actions with a continued rate cut cycle, we see a path for financial expense burdens to ease into 2024. Our Brazil economics team looks for a year-end 2024 benchmark interest rate of 10.0%, reaching 8.5% in September 2025. Altogether, rates are coming down, but at a slower pace than our economists expected as of mid-2023, and this slows the pace at which our covered companies can see an easing of financial expense headwinds. As a partial offset, we have seen steps from companies in our coverage to reduce debt levels — spanning asset sales, partnerships, and lower store openings; historical and forecast leverage levels across our Brazil coverage are included in Exhibit 125 . Altogether, we see financial expenses as a percentage of revenue at 2.7% on average across our Brazil-based coverage in 2024E; this marks an improvement from 4.7%

## in 2023E and 4.0% in 2022, but remains above the 2018-21 average of 2.0%.

Exhibit 125:Looking out to 2024E, we see discretionary retailers Casas Bahia & Magalu, and staples retailers Assai & CBD as the higher-leverage companies in our Brazil coverage, but with improvements from 2023E levels; Renner Petz, and Natura (post-Aesop sale) screen at the low-end

![](images/326110f9e5b3742ed2e070a5402bd0c0f02219f9f7f905d433c8d96d46e06035.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Our analysis shows electronics-first operators Magalu and Casas Bahia with high sensitivity to interest rates, on cyclical category exposure and current net margin pressures... Amid the interest rate debate, we look to 2024 earnings sensitivities based on changes in financial expenses across our Brazil coverage ( Exhibit 126 ). While interest rate changes may impact companies' retail sales or retail financial services operations, our analysis focuses solely on sensitizing net interest expense components — including yields on cash balances, receivables discounting charges, and interest expenses on debt. We note that sensitivity figures are relative to our current earnings estimates, which already incorporate lower benchmark rates with the cutting cycle having begun in August '23. Consistent with the market view around discretionary retailers' rate sensitivity, we see electronics-first operators Magazine Luiza and Casas Bahia with the most rate-sensitive net earnings profiles in our coverage. Despite exposure to categories with discretionary attributes, we see limited sensitivity to earnings for Lojas Renner, Petz, and Natura, due to low net debt (including the impact of Aesop sale proceeds for Natura).

... while we see rates exposure for cash & carry operators Assai and Carrefour Brasil, but with more self-help EBITDA levers. Despite exposure to the less-cyclical food staples end-market, we see ASAI3 and CRFB3 as rates-exposed names, as both companies took on transaction-related debt in recent years. We see a path to organic de-leverage as converted/acquired stores mature, and see a lift to earnings flow-through as policy rates decrease. For Assai, we look for end-2024E leverage of \~2.6x, with financial expenses as a % of revenue decreasing to 2.3% in '24E versus 2.6% in '22. For Carrefour Brasil, we see end-2024E leverage of 0.8x, with financial expenses at 1.8% of revenue (1.9% in 2022). Into 2024, we see the companies moving past peak conversion/integration headwinds, with uplifts from the maturity of converted Extra hypermarkets (Assai) and Grupo BIG stores (Carrefour Brasil). While the pace of Brazil benchmark rate cuts will ultimately impact the pace of interest rate relief, we continue to see directional improvements for both companies. Across our coverage more broadly, our leverage summary in Exhibit 127 points to further improvements on average into 2024.

Exhibit 126: At the company level, we see discretionary retailers Magalu & Casas Bahia with high earnings sensitivity to interest rates, followed by Assai & Carrefour Brasil (as staples retailers with financial leverage)

When considering the potential for tax changes in Brazil, we have comfort that our valuation frameworks are not predicated on favorable tax situations persisting. The Brazil government continues to pursue a tax reform agenda. For factors that could potentially impact the retail sector, we highlight dividend taxation, the removal of interest on capital (IOC) exemptions, and the inclusion of state tax benefits (ICMS) in the federal taxation base. As detailed in the LatAm Strategy team's Tax Reform Simulation note and in the LatAm Economics team's Challenging Race Ahead note, our covered retail companies were not among the most-impacted for dividend taxation or IOC. We have seen retailers in our coverage with state tax benefits, generally related to locating distribution (or manufacturing) capacity in certain areas. Assai and Renner each reported low tax rates in 2022 (8.6% for Assai and 6.7% for Renner), in part due to these state tax benefits. Carrefour Brasil also benefitted, but posted a higher (25%) tax rate. We see Magazine Luiza and Casas Bahia toward the upper end of our coverage for income tax benefits as a proportion of revenue, but with negative earnings before taxes in 2022; see our tax buildups in Exhibit 128 . In our estimates, we forecast average 2024E tax rates of \~29% across our Brazil coverage (range 15% to 41%) and 2027E tax rates of \~31% (range 24% to 36%) — demonstrating that despite benefits including in '22/'23, our long-term valuation

frameworks are not predicated on favorable tax situations persisting. While the tax reform agenda remains ongoing, we note that the removal of deductions could potentially be met with lower headline tax rates. If retailers do end up paying higher taxes, amid an already low-margin industry we believe the impact could be passed through to consumers over time (via pricing).

2024 Company Views, Risk-Reward Pages, and Model Summaries

## MercadoLibre (MELI.O, Overweight, PT \$2,020, Top Pick)

We highlight Brazil GMV, Mexico operations, and Argentina/FX as 3 items to watch for MELI in '24. With continued levers for profitability upside, OW-rated MELI remains our Top Pick. For Brazil GMV, we look for a managed deceleration, with MELI continuing to grow off the higher 2023 base including share gained from Americanas. Our +17% Brazil ex-FX GMV growth forecast for 2024E compares to +27% for 2023E and +21% in 2022. We look for Brazil results to demonstrate MELI's ability to gain share amid a more normalized operating backdrop for local peers, and to demonstrate resilience versus cross-border competition including a potential Temu Brazil entry. For Mexico operations, in the recent Morgan Stanley Latin America 2024 Big Debates report, we detailed why we believe Mexico can be MELI's 2024 story. With an increasingly built-out logistics network, under-penetrated Fintech opportunity, and improving contribution margin trends, we see scope for Mexico to be an increasingly outsized growth contributor for MELI in 2024 and the years ahead. We forecast Mexico ex-FX GMV increasing +20% in 2024E, with revenue increasing +38% (total company +20%), and contribution profits +44% y/y (total company +29%), with Mexico reaching 19% of total contribution profits. For Argentina/FX, we highlight macro uncertainty amid the new Milei administration, while MELI management including at our December 2023 Nasdaq Conference struck a positive tone around the potential for more pro-business policies to emerge. While FX distortions continued through 4Q23, following a mid-December peso devaluation the gap between official/ unofficial FX rates now stands at a more modest 44% (108% 2023 average), which should limit the magnitude of Argentina-related FX distortions in MELI's PNL for the year ahead. On the net income line, which is less-impacted by peso accounting effects, we forecast +71% y/y growth in 2024 (to US\$1.9 billion), for an 11.0% margin (+320bps y/y). Our price target of US\$2,020 represents +27% upside, and we see a 1.2:1.0 bull-bear skew.

## US\$2,020.00PRICE TARGET

Our end-2024 price target is derived from a DCF analysis which assumes 13.3% cost of capital, 6.5% terminal growth rate, and 27.6% terminal EBIT margins for the whole company. PT implies 4.6x 2025e EV/Revenue and 19.4x 2025e EV/EBITDA for the whole business.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ MELI is a LatAm eCommerce and ntech leader. We see the company well positioned to capture the secular growth opportunities on both fronts, supported by an integrated product and services ecosystem.
▪ On eCommerce, we see a setup for share gain. Further, we believe MELI can increase marketplace monetization through
expanding value-added services including advertising and logistics.
▪ On <sub>fi</sub>ntech, we see opportunity for volume growth across core payment processing and mobile wallet verticals, along with the
ability to monetize through additional
services such as credit.
▪ On pro tability, our business line analysis shows a breadth of drivers that can support continued margin expansion.

## BULL CASE

## Pulling Away

US\$2,400.00

MELI's platform takes hold, enabling accelerated monetization across both ntech and commerce. Bull Case implies an +20% online GMV USD CAGR (+43% ex-FX) between 2023e-2026e, with a + 21% USD CAGR in Brazil, +13% in Argentina and +26% in Mexico. Total TPV USD CAGR of +26%, with off-platform transactions reaching 77% of the total. Revenue grows at a +29% CAGR (USD), while EBITDA margin expands +960bps to 28.8% in 2026e. Bull Case is based on 4.8x EV / 2025e Bull Case Revenue.

## BASE CASE

## Staying Ahead

## US\$2,020.00 BEAR CASE

MELI further extends its lead in eCommerce and has success in growing new payments businesses, driving pro table growth. Base Case implies a +11% online GMV USD CAGR (+33% ex-FX) between 2023e-2026e, with a +11% USD CAGR in Brazil, +7% in Argentina and +16% in Mexico. Total TPV USD CAGR of +21%, with off-platform transactions reaching 79% of the total by 2026e. Revenue grows at a +20% CAGR (USD), while EBITDA margin expands +720bps to 26.5%.

US\$940.00

## Competition Catching Up

MELI faces intensifying competition and macro headwinds, pressuring growth and margins. Bear Case implies a +2% online GMV USD CAGR (+22% ex-FX) between 2023e-2026e, with a +1% USD CAGR in Brazil, -1% in Argentina and +9% in Mexico. Total TPV USD CAGR of +14%, with offplatform transactions reaching 81% of the total. Revenue grows at a +7% CAGR (USD), while EBITDA margin contracts -460bps to 14.0% in 2026e. Bear Case is based on 2.7x EV / 2025e Bear Case Revenue.

## Magazine Luiza (MGLU3.SA, Underweight, PT R\$2.0)

We highlight online GMV, new bets traction, and net margin flow-through as 3 items to watch for Magalu in '24. With incremental caution around eCommerce trends and the ability of newer initiatives to support a margin inflection, we downgrade to Underweight (from Equal-weight). For online GMV, Magalu fell short of our initial expectations regarding GMV re-capture from Americanas. We see '24 with some tailwinds, including a move past the DIFAL tax drag and the impact of lower interest rates. Our forecasts imply a pickup in first-party GMV (to +11% y/y in 2024E, from -1% for 2023E), and a slight deceleration in marketplace (+19% in 2024E, from +21% for 2023E). Overall, we look for +15% online GMV growth in 2024E, and +12% total growth when including stores, while we see limited scope for a sharper upward inflection. For new bets traction, Magalu brought a cloud business into focus with the December 2023 Magalu Cloud launch event, and the company's Chief Technology Officer spoke to the impact of artificial intelligence on Magalu overall in our August 2023 call. Disclosures on Magalu ads have increased, but we see limited sign of PNL traction in other tangential business lines such as food delivery and digital banking, following a multiyear broadening into newer areas (including through M&A). Awaiting greater disclosure, we remain cautious around the potential for new bets to have an uplift on Magalu's financials. For net margin flowthrough, we see a directional easing of headwinds as benchmark interest rates decrease, but our base case estimates imply Magalu only slightly exceeds breakeven in 2024. On 2024E EBITDA margins +110bps y/y (to 7.0%, post IFRS-16), we forecast net margins +170bps y/y (to 0.1%). Into 2025E, even when incorporating further EBITDA improvement and interest expense easing, we forecast Magalu at a still-modest 1.0% net margin. Our stock rating goes to Underweight (from Equal-weight), as lower conviction in the pace of a top-down recovery and in marketplace growth leaves Magalu with limited valuation support (NM P/E for '24E, and 31x on our '25E figures). Absent a return to upward estimate revisions, we see a risk-reward setup consistent with an Underweight rating. Our price target goes to R\$2.0 (from R\$2.75), implying 7.3x '25E EBITDA / 28x '25E EPS / 14x '26E EPS, on lower '25+ EBIT margin forecasts amid limited traction in longer-tail categories in our AlphaWise survey.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Magazine Luiza is an omni-channel retailer with a leading operation for 1P electronics. The company continues to scale up its 3P marketplace and adjacent service offerings, including through acquisitions.

▪ Balancing digital optionality, Magalu's core stores and 1P eCommerce operations remain concentrated in the electronics segment; the combination of cyclical category headwinds, high interest rates, and digital costs have dragged on margins.

▪ We are constructive on Magalu's track record of online growth, but we see category exposure remaining a headwind and see earnings ow-through remaining pressured absent evidence of value-added services feeding through to margins.

## BULL CASE

## Digitalizing Brazil

R\$3.25

MGLU's platform expansion including highermargin services comes faster than expected; a cyclical rebound supports growth reinvestment. Bull Case implies a 12% stores' revenue CAGR in 2023e-2026e, with 8% average SSS growth and a 3% area CAGR. Online GMV grows at a 20% CAGR over the period, reaching 76% of the total. Net revenue grows at a 15% CAGR, while adjusted EBITDA margin expands +500bps, reaching 11.3% in 2026e. Bull Case is based on 18x 2025e P/E.

## BASE CASE

## Limited Growth Beyond the Core

MGLU grows its core eCommerce business, but cyclical headwinds pressure store/1P sales and margins, and newer categories/services provide a limited offset. Base Case implies a 7% stores' revenue CAGR in 2023e-2026e, with 5% average SSS growth and 2% area CAGR. Online GMV grows at a 15% CAGR over the period, reaching 76% of the total. Net revenue grows at an 11% CAGR, while adjusted EBITDA margin expands +200bps to 7.9% in 2026e.

## R\$2.00 BEAR CASE

R\$1.00

## Retail & eCommerce Deceleration

MGLU's tech transition falters, and store/cyclical headwinds pressure top and bottom-line results. Bear Case implies a 4% store revenue CAGR in 2023e-2026e, with 3% average SSS growth and a 1% area CAGR. Online GMV grows at a +11% CAGR over the period, reaching 75% of the total. Net revenue grows at an 8% CAGR, while adjusted EBITDA margin expands +10bps, reaching 5.6% in 2026e. Bear Case is based on 7.5x 2025e EV/EBITDA.

## Casas Bahia (BHIA3.SA, Underweight, PT R\$10)

We highlight overall GMV trends, net margin flow-through, and transformation plan progress as 3 items to watch for Casas Bahia in '24. With limited visibility on the path to positive net income, we are UW-rated. For overall GMV trends, we see Casas Bahia tracking to a -0.5% y/y decline in 2023E, with cyclical goods headwinds and a focus on profitability initiatives hindering Casas Bahia's ability to capture share ceded by Americanas. Into 2024E, we forecast a modest +5% y/y increase in GMV, with stores +1% and online +11%; lower interest rates could be a tailwind, but transformation plan initiatives including store closures and the migration of certain categories to 3P could remain a drag. For net margin flow-through, we forecast net margins of -2.1% in 2024E. Lower interest rates can provide some relief — we look for net financial results of -6.1% of revenue, versus -8.1% in 2023E. However, with our forecast for Adj. EBITDA margins of 8.1% in 2024E (post-IFRS16), we believe the path to net profits remains challenged. For transformation plan progress, along with 3Q earnings management highlighted an incremental up to R\$500 million in new opportunities around services revenue and indirect costs (for a total of R\$1.5-1.6 billion at the EBT level, or \~5% vs our 2023E revenue). Focus areas have included store/DC closures, inventory/capex reductions, a \~R \$620 million follow-on, and the launch of a FIDC. Our price target goes to R\$10 (from R \$11.4), on lower terminal EBIT margin forecasts amid limited traction in longer-tail categories in our AlphaWise survey. We see potential for equity value volatility with Casas Bahia's market capitalization representing just 13% of the company's enterprise value by our calculation. Amid operating and financial expense pressures, we are UW-rated.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Casas Bahia (formerly Via) faces a challenged backdrop in its key categories of electronics, appliances, and furniture in Brazil, amid top-down headwinds and the continued rise of eCommerce pure-play competition.

▪ We see headwinds persisting for core durable goods categories, while 3P marketplace GMV has been pressured as take rates increase, and we believe visibility on the sales/margin balance remains low. ▪ While a greater category rebound and expense ef ciencies could support margin upside in a bull case, and Casas Bahia's nancial leverage drives a wide bull-bear range, with our base case of pressured market share and bottom line ow-through we are Underweight-rated.

![](images/c6ee0cc212dd80a3f549e18d6e5b8c845ea38c7e5b73b2724f0d12dd3738c1ca.jpg)

## BULL CASE

## Digital Progress Broadens

## R\$18.00 BASE CASE

Casas Bahia is successful in expanding its omni-channel model into new product and services verticals. Bull Case implies +4% B&M revenue CAGR over 2023-26e, with +5% average SSS growth and +1% area CAGR. Online GMV grows at a 15% CAGR over the period, reaching 53% of the total by 2026e. Net Revenue grows at an 8% CAGR, while EBITDA margin expands +390bps, reaching 10.1% in 2026e. Bull Case is based on 9.0x 2025e P/E .

## Core Category Headwinds

Casas Bahia retains a scaled position within durable goods retail, but macro headwinds weigh on the category and drive a slow deleveraging path. Base Case implies +3% B&M revenue CAGR over 2023-26e, with +4% average SSS growth, and 1% area CAGR. Online GMV grows at a +11% CAGR over the period, reaching 51% of the total by 2026e. Net Revenue grows at a 6% CAGR, while EBITDA margin expands +290bps, reaching 8.8% in 2026e.

## R\$10.00 BEAR CASE

R\$2.50

## Increasing Margin Pressure

Extended macro headwinds and ramping competition pressure Casas Bahia's store and online operations, leading to margin deleverage and extended net losses. Bear Case implies +1% B&M revenue CAGR over 2023- 26e, with +2% average SSS growth, and +1% area CAGR. Online GMV increases at a +8% CAGR over the period, reaching 50% of the total by 2026e. Net Revenue grows at a 4% CAGR, while EBITDA margin expands +280bps, reaching 8.7% in 2025e. Bear Case is based on 2.4x EV / 2025e Bear Case EBITDA.

## Meliuz SA (CASH3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$10)

We highlight Brazil commerce, BV partnership, and return of capital as 3 items to watch for Meliuz in '24. While return of capital provides downside protection, amid operating volatility we are EW-rated. For Brazil commerce, we see marketplace revenue on track for a -3% y/y decline in 2023E, with momentum stalling following a \~10x increase from 2017-2022. We see Brazil eCommerce platforms focusing on margins over growth, potentially weighing on spend for affiliate/cash-back partners such as Meliuz. We look for Meliuz's Brazil eCommerce revenue to return to growth in 2024E, at +16% y/y. but visibility is low following four quarters of declines. For the BV partnership, Meliuz has begun offering financial services in partnership with Banco BV, with '24 launch plans of products including deposit certificates (CDBs) and PIX in installments. While we favor the move to a more asset-light financial services strategy, along with the sale of Banco Acesso, visibility for financial services TPV and revenue is low amid this transition period. For return of capital, Meliuz in November 2023 concluded the transaction with Banco BV for the sale of Acesso. Meliuz's board has approved a capital reduction of R\$210 million (\~32% of market cap), which as of the company's 3Q23 earnings release management expects to occur in 1H24. Our price target of R\$10 represents 26% upside, and we see a highly positive 5.5:1.0 risk-reward skew amid downside protection from capital return. While with limited operating and financial visibility amid three years (2021 through 2023E) with negative EBITDA and net income margins, we maintain our Equal-weight rating.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Meliuz is a platform-agnostic play on Brazil eCommerce, driving traf c to platforms while providing cash-back to consumers. A competitive eCommerce backdrop supports demand for Meliuz's services, but platforms' increasing margin focus can be a monetization headwind. ▪ Meliuz has expanded into international operations (through the Picodi acquisition) and into financial services (through the Acesso acquisition), while traction in both areas is earlier-stage and has contributed to margin pressure. ▪ We see limited visibility on the normalized sales/margin outlook, while a strategic alliance with a nancial services partner provides an element of support.

![](images/acb0fa41987af71b89b07a3c215631f50c58dcf91351ead14d09e5b999d4c4e4.jpg)

## R\$10.00 BEAR CASE

Meliuz expands the user base in Brazil and rolls out the model to international markets through Picodi, with early-stage cross-selling of nancial services; however, expansion initiatives weigh on margins. Brazil GMV grows at a +13% CAGR between 2023e and 2026e, while International GMV grows at +18%; net revenue grows at +13% in the period, with EBITDA margin reaching 5.4% in 2026E.

# Americanas SA (AMER3.SA, Not-Rated)

We highlight financial restructuring progress, strategic initiatives, and signs of a GMV recovery as 3 items to watch for Americanas in '24. For financial restructuring process, in December 2023 Americanas' creditors approved the company's debt restructuring plan, with Americanas having entered into judicial recovery in January 2023 following findings of accounting inconsistencies and ultimately fraud. As detailed in What We Learned from Americanas' 2022 Results (16 Nov 2023), the restructuring plan includes a recapitalization, potentially including R\$12 billion of capitalized debt and a R\$12 billion capital injection from the reference shareholders. For strategic initiatives, management will focus on cash flow generation and profitability, including better capturing synergies across retail channels. In the physical stores, AMER will focus on tailoring assortments at the store level to better serve clients in each location. In online, the company will narrow its assortment, focusing on categories more aligned with the physical store mix. Americanas bridge to reach guided R\$2.2 billion of EBITDA (>R\$1.5 billion pre-IFRS16) in 2025 includes multiple positive levers around organic growth, store renovations, purchasing/pricing, greater financial services revenue, and incremental digital profitability — which we believe could prove difficult to simultaneously execute on. For a GMV recovery, after our estimate of a \~70% decline in online merchandise volumes in 2023, Americanas enters 2024 with a highly depressed comparison base and a soon-to-be restructured balance sheet. While this could support a return to stabilization / growth, amid step-downs in app trends and in our survey data, we do not expect a meaningful snap-back in Americanas Brazil eCommerce market share (est. 3% for 2024E, versus 3% for 2023E and 14% for 2022). We remain Not-Rated on Americanas with still-limited updated financial disclosure amid the ongoing restructuring.

## Lojas Renner (LREN3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$18)

We highlight retail EBITDA, financial services results, and tax/policy changes as 3 items to watch for Renner in '24. With value versus history but limited catalyst for upward revisions, we are EW-rated. For retail EBITDA, we forecast +22% y/y growth in '24E, to R\$2.6 billion, while amid recent-years' volatility this represents a modest +5% CAGR from 2022 levels, and a +6% CAGR from the 2019/pre-pandemic baseline. We see gross margin tailwinds from lower input costs, and operating tailwinds from the new DC (including moving past peak double-running costs); for detail, see our October 2023 NDR Takeaways. While we see industry price competition, and continued digital investment needs, keeping Renner with a >3pp gap to pre-pandemic EBITDA margins in '24. For financial services results, following four consecutive negative quarters, we see Renner tracking to a \~R\$(70 million) EBITDA contribution loss from financial services in 2023 (though management in 3Q highlighted that September had returned to positive). We look for improving past-dues in part reflecting more restrictive credit policies, and forecast financial services EBITDA returning positive at \~R\$120 million in 2024E — while this represents just 4% of total EBITDA, below the \~20% levels from 2017-2019. For tax/ policy changes, amid cross-border competition we believe changes in import taxation related to the Remessa Conforme program are a key item to watch; further taxes on top of the current 17% state-level tax could further level the playing field in favor of local operators. As one of the companies in our coverage with the lowest tax rates, Renner further has earnings sensitivity around changes to interest on capital and state-level subvention benefit taxation. Our price target of R\$18 represents +9% upside and implies 16x 2024E / 13.5x 2025E earnings; while this is below Renner's historical average of 21x, we see retail and financial services competition weighing on the potential for a re-rating.

## Risk Reward - Lojas Renner<sub>Risk Reward – Lojas Renner SA (LREN3.SA)</sub>

Brazil Apparel Leadership, While Cost of Growth Headwinds Appear Priced In

## R\$18.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from a BRL-denominated DCF that assumes 14.5% cost of capital, 5.5% terminal growth rate, and 13.9% terminal adj. EBIT margins. PT implies 13.5x 2025e P/E.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Renner is a best-in-class apparel retailer in Brazil, with private brand products and an integrated supply chain as differentiators. Within this fragmented market, we believe Renner has room to increase market share from current +HSD levels.
▪ Despite a share gain outlook, we are
cautious on costs of growth, particularly within Renner's digital retail and nancial services business lines. More broadly, we see a competitive set expanding from physical apparel peers, now also including
marketplace and cross-border operators. ▪ Balancing trading multiples near multiyear lows with still-limited visibility on the normalized margin outlook post-Covid, we are EW-rated.

## BULL CASE

## Pro table Share Gains

R\$24.00

Renner's omni-channel initiatives further widen the operating spread versus peers, enabling accelerating share gains with margin ow-through. Bull Case implies retail revenue grows at a +10% CAGR over 2023- 2026e, with comps growing +8% on average, and selling area increasing at a +3% CAGR over the period. Retail EBITDA margin expands +470bps to 23.4% in 2026e. Financial services reaches \~14% of total EBITDA in 2026e. Bull Case value is based on 13x P/E on our 2025e Bull Case estimates.

## BASE CASE

## Higher Costs of Growth

## R\$18.00

Renner continues to grow sales as the retail backdrop normalizes, while the accelerated digital shift and ramping competition pressures retail and nancial services pro tability. Base Case implies retail revenue grows at a +10% CAGR over 2023- 2026e, with comps growing +5% on average, and selling area increasing at a +4% CAGR over the period. Retail EBITDA margin expands +300bps, reaching 21.4% in 2026e. Financial services accounts for \~7% of total EBITDA in 2026e.

## BEAR CASE

R\$11.00

## Physical and Digital Pressure

Incremental market share gains prove harder than expected. Renner faces macro/traf c headwinds and greater digital competition, impacting sales and margins. Bear Case implies retail revenue increases at a +3% CAGR over 2023-2026e, with comps increasing +2% on average, and selling area increasing at a +2% CAGR over the period. Retail EBITDA margin expands +50bps to 18.6% in 2026e. Bear Case value is based on \~11x P/E on our 2025e Bear Case estimates.

## C&A Modas (CEAB3.SA, Underweight, PT R\$5.5)

We highlight retail revenue, C&A Pay performance, and net margin flow-through as 3 items to watch for C&A in '24. Execution exceeded our expectations in '23, but with this more than reflected in the share price, in our view, we stay UW-rated. For retail revenue, we forecast +7% y/y growth in 2024E, for a +6% CAGR vs. 2019 levels. Tracking to +5% y/y for 2023E, C&A notably outpaced Renner's +0.5% growth, while this only partially closes the gap versus 2019 levels (Renner +37% vs '19 for 2023E, and C&A +23%). We look for a return to modest unit growth (MSe +15 stores in 2024E, for +4.5% y/y expansion), with forecast comps of +6.3%. For C&A Pay performance, the private label ramp-up has been met with consumer traction, reaching 22% of the company's retail sales as of 3Q23, with \~4.2 million cards issued. We estimate a R\$(101mn) private label loss in 2023E and a similar R\$(86mn) in 2024E, while this doesn't account for the incremental profit contribution of merchandise sales via owned credit. Including the legacy Bradesco partnership, we see C&A 26% in-house card penetration for 3Q23, up from 21% (Bradescoonly) in 2019, narrowing the gap versus Renner at 29% in-house card penetration for 3Q23. Following the sharpest ramp-up period, we see less scope for incremental retail sales driven by C&A Pay in '24 versus '23. For net margin flow-through, we see C&A with \~R\$990 million of adjusted EBITDA for 2023E, +4% above 2019 levels though with \~300bps lower margins. While despite exceeding our EBITDA estimates over the last year, we see C&A tracking to \~R\$(50 million) of net losses for 2023E, a -0.8% margin. We look for a +0.6% net margin in 2024E; while amid industry competition and net financial results at a still-high 5% of net revenue for 2024E, we believe scope for a more meaningful net profit increase remains limited. Our price target remains R\$5.5, representing -26% downside; while C&A has disproven initial parts of our UW fundamental thesis with the 2023 sales/margin expansion cadence, we believe these improvements are overly reflected in the current 54x 2024E and 21x 2025E P/E multiples on our estimates. We await greater net profit upside, or a more balanced risk-reward skew, to revisit our rating.

## R\$5.50PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from a BRL-denominated DCF analysis with a 15.4% cost of capital, 5.0% terminal growth rate, and 6.8% terminal EBIT margins. PT implies 2.3x 2025 EV/EBITDA.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ With \~330 stores and \~5% market share, C&A is among the top apparel players in Brazil. Key company-speci <sup>fi</sup>c initiatives span unit growth, remodels, distribution, credit (C&A Pay), and digital/eCommerce. ▪ We are constructive on operating initiatives, but see margin headwinds from eCommerce costs and physical/digital investments persisting post-pandemic. With both elevated operating and nancial expenses (from high interest rates), we see bottom line pressure persisting. ▪ Coupling our cautious industry stance within Brazil apparel and a sales/margin recovery that has lagged its top peer, we are Underweight-rated.

![](images/cc627b699ea98fdaf8010868e9fc7cb7911e0d39f1862a08542034f7682fdc5c.jpg)

## BULL CASE

## C&A Re-accelerates

C&A executes on its top line initiatives and is able to reap the bene t to margins as well. Bull case implies a 9% 2023-26e revenue CAGR (6% store growth, 7% comps) and a 14% adjusted EBITDA CAGR (+220bps margin expansion to 17.4% in 2026e). Bull case is based on 4.5x 2025 EV/EBITDA.

## R\$12.00 BASE CASE

## R\$5.50

## C&A Margin Recovery Lags

C&A's store growth and digital initiatives drive incremental sales, but associated operating and nancial expenses limit bottom line ow-through. Base case implies <sub>fi</sub>a 8% 2023-26e revenue CAGR (5% store growth, 6% comps) and a 12% adjusted EBITDA CAGR (+190bps margin expansion to 17.0% in 2026e). Base case implies 2.3x 2025 EV/EBITDA.

## BEAR CASE

## C&A Struggles

R\$3.00

C&A faces execution issues on its initiatives, plus persistent macro, competition and nancial services headwinds, and growth comes with incremental margin pressure. Bear case implies a % 2023-26e revenue CAGR (4% store growth, 5% comps) and a 9% adjusted EBITDA CAGR (+120bps margin expansion to 16.0% in 2026e). Bear case is based on 2.9x 2025 EV/EBITDA.

## Assai / Sendas Distribuidora S.A. (ASAI3.SA, Overweight, PT R\$20)

We highlight comp trends, net income flow-through, and taxation changes as 3 items to watch for Assai in '24. Following pressure in '23, we see top- to bottom-line tailwinds for the year ahead, keeping us OW-rated. For comp trends, we look for Assai to end 2023 with -1% comps, marking the worst result for the company in 10+ years (2013-2022 average SSS of +12.4%). As discussed in the Brazil Food Retail section, a move to broad-based deflation was a drag. However, our Global Discounters analysis supports our view that headwinds were cyclical, not structural, with cash & carry likely to remain a growth engine for Brazil food retail. We look for a rebound to +12.6% comps for Assai in 2024, with lesser deflation in 1H, a return to inflation in 2H, and a maturity lift from converted Extra hypermarkets benefitting results through the year. For net margin flow-through, on EBITDA margins -20bps y/y (to 6.9%) for 2023E, net margins are tracking to contract a more substantial -120bps y/y (to 1.0%). The headwind was largely from greater financial expenses, both with higher benchmark rates impacting the cost of Extra-related debt, and also on greater lease interest from converted stores. Looking to 2024E, we see relief from declining benchmark rates, and less room for Extra-related surprises as the conversion project is now largely behind us. On EBITDA margins up \~35bps y/y (to 7.2%) we forecast net margins +50bps to 1.5% for 2024E, further expanding to 2.1% for 2025E. For taxation changes, Assai's aggregate tax rate has been \~15% between 2019-2023E. We forecast taxes stepping up to 28% for 2024E, with our model implying a loss of \~75% of tax subvention benefits. Amid ongoing uncertainty we keep a conservative approach, while more favorable policies could prove a source of upside. Our price target of R\$20 implies +43% upside. As we move past a cycle of negative estimate revisions, we believe the current multiples of 16x 2024E and 10x 2025E earnings on our estimates screen as attractive relative to our growth outlook.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Sendas Distribuidora (Assai) is a pure-play on Brazil's growing cash & carry food retail segment. Stores serve both household and business consumers, with bulk-sized goods as a differentiating factor versus other food retail channels.

▪ We see room for the cash & carry segment to continue outgrowing overall Brazil food retail, with a shift to value formats in both B2C and B2B channels.

▪ We see growth from both above-in ation comps and from unit expansion, furthered with the Extra Hiper stores deal. On a growth-adjusted basis we see attractive value.

![](images/315769383ccc0fa11ecd490c767f92d744c8662c827fd4f30de44a52721d5d1c.jpg)

## BULL CASE

R\$23.00

## Long Runway for Cash & Carry

The store growth opportunity accelerates and scale gains drive margin expansion, supporting a share price re-rating. Bull Case implies revenue grows at a +17% CAGR over 2023e-2026e (including Extra hypermarkets conversions), with comps growing +11% on average and selling area increasing at a +7% CAGR over the period. EBITDA margin expands +100bps to 7.9%. Bull Case value is based on \~14x P/E on our 2025e Bull Case estimates.

BASE CASE

R\$20.00

## Cash & Carry Growth, 'Extra' Upside

Assai executes on its store growth strategy, including through the Extra deal, while the C&C segment continues to see secular tailwinds. Base Case implies revenue grows at a +14% CAGR over 2023e-2026e (including Extra hypermarkets conversions), with comps growing +9% on average and selling area increasing at a +6% CAGR over the period. EBITDA margin expands +60bps, reaching 7.5% in 2026e.

## BEAR CASE

R\$8.00

## Competition Pressures the Cash & Carry Algorithm

Competition in the channel drives less unit expansion potential for Assai, along with greater pricing/promotional pressure. Bear Case implies revenue grows at a +9% CAGR over 2023e-2026e (including Extra hypermarkets conversions), with comps growing +6% on average and selling area increasing at a +3% CAGR over the period. EBITDA margin remains stable at 6.8% through 2026e. Bear Case value is based on 9x P/E on our 2025e Bear Case estimates.

## CBD (PCAR3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$4.5)

We highlight GPA Brazil margins, asset sale progress, and the financial de-leverage path as 3 items to watch for CBD in '24. We favor the structural simplification efforts, but amid pressured net income flow-through we remain EW-rated. For GPA Brazil margins, at its December 2023 Investor Day CBD reiterated its target of 8-9% GPA Brazil EBITDA margins, initially announced at its December 2022 event. Key drivers include further breakage level reduction along with higher promotion efficiencies, and increasing sales productivity (leading to operating leverage). While versus 6.7% margins for 2022, we see CBD tracking to just 6.9% GPA Brazil EBITDA margins for 2023E. While we credit an improvement to 8.1% in 2024E with a lagged effect of recent initiatives, we are cautious around the potential for CBD to achieve a result closer to the high-end of the guidance range. For asset sale progress, 2023 included the spin-off of Grupo Exito, and the subsequent sale of Exito to Grupo Calleja. Following past-years' transactions, including spinning off Assai and exiting the hypermarket segment, we see the Exito transactions leaving GPA with a more streamlined set of Brazil food retail operating assets. At the investor day, management cited expectations for \~R\$850 million of cash inflows from the sale of Exito and Cnova, along with additional proceeds from executing other non-core asset sales, including the headquarters' real estate monetization and sale of gas stations. For financial de-leverage, CBD management sees the business with 5.9x leverage as of 3Q23, bridging to 3.9x post asset sales (ex. operating non-core assets) and upon achieving the midpoint of the EBITDA margin guidance. We forecast a net financial result of -4.5% of revenue for 2024E, versus EBITDA margins of 8.1%; in our view, this demonstrates the importance of de-leveraging for any potential return to positive net income. In December 2023, CBD further announced that they are studying a potential primary offer, to further aid the de-leveraging path. Our price target represents +23% upside, while we see an unfavorable 0.9:1.0 bull-bear skew as financial leverage limits bear case downside support.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ CBD's GPA Brazil is a top food retailer in the country, with operations spanning supermarkets, convenience-focused formats, and digital operations. ▪ Outside of core Brazil operations, CBD has been streamlining assets including the Assai spin-off in 2021, hypermarket exit in 2022, and the Exito spin-off in 2023. After spinning off Exito, which contains banners across Colombia, Uruguay, and Argentina, CBD's operations are Brazil-focused. ▪ We view positively CBD's steps to streamline its portfolio, with further asset value cyrstallization from the announced Cnova and Exito stake sales, but amid a stillhigh debt burden and awaiting core margin improvements, we are EW-rated.

![](images/6ea4b77f4c60d9581fb7ed900a06fb20e9a69a40d249399f974b001bb85dd81a.jpg)

## BULL CASE

## Margin In ection plus Asset Value

## R\$6.00 BASE CASE

CBD executes on improving its GPA Brazil <sup>fl</sup>banners, and delivers upside in asset monetization. Net Revenue increases at a +5% CAGR over 2023e-2026e, with comps growing on average +6% in GPA Brazil. Total selling area increases at a +2% CAGR in the period. Consolidated EBITDA margin expands +180bps to 8.8% in 2026e. Bull Case is based on 3.3x EV/EBITDA pre IFRS16 on our Bull Case 2025e estimates.

## Portfolio Streamlining; Await Margin Execution

CBD streamlines its portfolio including through the Exito spin-off, and the focus shifts to executing on Brazil initiatives across digital, private label, and loyalty. Net Revenue increases at a +4% CAGR over 2023e-2026e, with comps at +5% on average in GPA Brazil. Total selling area increases at a +1% CAGR in the period. Adjusted EBITDA margin expands +150bps to 8.4% in 2026e.

## R\$4.50 BEAR CASE

R\$1.00

## Business Transformations Face Headwinds

Remodels and digital initiatives don't yield expected results, and an inability to monetize assets drives elevated leverage. Net Revenue increases at a +3% CAGR over 2023e-2026e, with average comps of +5% in GPA Brazil. Total selling area declines at a -1% CAGR in the period. Consolidated EBITDA margin expands +120bps, reaching 8.0% in 2026e. Bear Case is based on 2.7x EV/EBITDA pre IFRS16 on our Bear Case 2025e estimates.

## Carrefour Brasil / Atacadao (CRFB3.SA, Overweight, PT R \$16.5)

We highlight Atacadao comps, BIG integration progress, and net margin flow-through as 3 items to watch for Carrefour Brasil in '24. Anticipating sales & margin tailwinds for the year ahead, we are OW-rated. For Atacadao comps, we see a similar setup to Assai, with '23 pressure (est. Atacadao comps of -0.6%) setting up for a favorable '24 backdrop (est. Atacadao comps of +8.7%) — as deflationary pressures ease, and converted stores see a maturity uplift. Our sales analysis by store cohort supports our conviction in the path to sales improvements. For BIG Integration Progress, we believe Carrefour has moved past the peak periods of sales and margin disruption, namely with store conversions and migrating banking consumers from BIG to CSF Bank. At its December 2023 Investor Day, management reiterated a converted Atacadao maturation curve of 6-9% EBITDA margins by 36+ months of operation. Management expects end-'24 converted store margins of 5.0-6.0% — for a \~20-40bps tailwind by our calculation, as \~18% of sales area could see this maturation lift in the year ahead. Synergy flow-through remains a tailwind as well; at the investor day management reiterated the R\$2 billion annual target by end-2025, while with R\$1.5 billion reached already by 3Q23. Altogether, we look for peak store conversion and deal integration headwinds to be behind us. For net margin flow-through, integration disruption effects and the impact of higher benchmark rates on deal-related interest have driven net margins of just 0.1% for 2023E, below 1.8% in 2022 and 4.3% in 2021. We look for a partial rebound to 1.3% in 2024E, and another year of outsized bottom line growth with 2.0% net margins in 2025E. We note Carrefour continues to contemplate a real estate carve-out plan; the proposed structure includes 1-3 minority investors taking a combined <49% stake in a "RentCo", with \~235 stores and a reiterated \~R\$1.5 billion of net operating income. Potentially, a transaction could free up capital for de-leveraging of higher cost debt. Our price target of R\$16.5 represents +42% upside. Similarly to Assai, as we move past a cycle of negative estimate revisions, we believe the current multiples of 17x 2024E and 10x 2025E earnings on our estimates screen as attractive relative to our growth outlook.

## Risk Reward - Atacadao S<sub>Risk Reward – Atacadao S.A. (CRFB3.SA)</sub>

Cash & Carry Leader at an Attractive Value; BIG Integration In-Focus

## R\$16.50PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from a SOTP with the retail businesses (Atacadao + Carrefour Retail + BIG) and CSF. Retail businesses value is derived from a BRL-denominated DCF that assumes 14.1% cost of capital, 4.5% terminal growth rate, and 4.4% terminal adj. EBIT margins. CSF value is derived from a BRL-denominated DDM. Our PT implies \~15x 2025e P/E.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Carrefour Brasil is the leader in Brazil cash & carry retail, a bulk purchase, value-focused grocery segment where we see secular growth. Retail stores (primarily hypermarkets) and <sup>fi</sup>nancial services round out the business.

▪ The multi-year cash & carry shift has been a tailwind, and we see segment growth continuing; while we see a more muted outlook for hypermarkets and nancial services, valuation screens attractive for the overall business.

▪ We lean positive on the Grupo BIG deal, with complementary regional exposure, Sam's Club expansion optionality, and synergy potential, while greater near-term re-investment needs are a risk.

![](images/755a769d08c9de9c15e72d841a5fc3bee004f62c85090dc7e209a885ef4456b1.jpg)

## BULL CASE

R\$20.00

## C&C Powers Through; BIG Upside Optionality

BASE CASE

CRFB's Cash & Carry banner Atacadao continues posting sales and margin momentum, while BIG store conversions and synergy ow-through drive a Retail format re-acceleration. Bull Case implies a +13% 2023-26E pro-forma revenue CAGR (14% Atacadao, 7% Retail, 9% CSF) and an +20% pro-forma adj. EBITDA CAGR (21% Atacadao, 34% Retail, 1% CSF), with +140bps proforma margins expansion to 7.7%. Bull Case implies \~14x 2025e P/E (on Bull Case estimates).

## Steady C&C Growth; Complementary BIG Operations

Atacadao bene ts as the scale leader in the cash & carry industry, with the Grupo BIG <sub>fi</sub>transaction supporting growth over time from store conversions and deal synergies. Base Case implies an +10% 2023-26E proforma revenue CAGR (11% Atacadao, 4% Retail, 6% CSF) and a +22% pro-forma adj. EBITDA CAGR (18% Atacadao, 32% Retail, 18% CSF), with +200bps pro-forma margin expansion to 7.4%.

## R\$16.50 BEAR CASE

R\$7.00

## Hypermarket Headwinds Overpower

Growth decelerates in all formats, with pressure including macro and in ation headwinds negatively impacting results, particularly at hypermarkets. Bear Case implies a +6% 2023-26E pro-forma revenue CAGR (7% Atacadao, 0% Retail, 2% CSF) and +16% pro-forma adj. EBITDA CAGR (12% Atacadao, 24% Retail), with pro-forma margins expanding +160bps to 5.8% in '26E. Bear case also considers unpaid provisions by the end of our forecast period. Bear Case implies \~9x 2025e P/E (on Bear Case estimates).

Natura Consensus EBITDA Revisions

## Natura & Co (NTCO3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$17)

We highlight Wave 2 LatAm integration, Avon International trends, and capital structure optimization as 3 items to watch for Natura in '24. Awaiting greater visibility on operating trends, we are EW-rated. For Wave 2 LatAm integration, the Natura & Avon brands are being further aligned, including cross-selling by consultants. In 2023, the roll-out drove top line and margin headwinds in Peru, Colombia, and Brazil, with management citing the impact of operational preparations and portfolio optimization (including in home & style). However, commentary on productivity improvements in Peru and Colombia were constructive, and in Brazil management noted cross-sell and productivity are already benefitting from the roll-out. For Natura & Co LatAm, we forecast 2024E revenue -2%, with adjusted EBITDA +5%, accelerating to revenue +4% / EBITDA +19% for 2025E. For Avon International, operating trends have been pressured, with 2023E net revenues tracking -31% below 2021 levels, though with broadly stable EBITDA margins versus 2021. Cash conversion and contribution margins are focus areas for management, and we credit the recent-quarters' profitability trend, but remain cautious on the segment outlook amid sales declines. Natura continues to evaluate areas such as partnerships and country sales, while we see a pivot away from prior commentary around the potential for a set of major market closures. For capital structure optimization, Natura ended 3Q with a net cash position (following the Aesop sale), and on December 29 2023 Natura announced the conclusion of the sale of The Body Shop to Aurelius (at an enterprise value of \~GBP210 million, including earn-outs). Natura ended the quarter with - 0.3x Net Cash/LTM adj. EBITDA pre-IFRS16 (versus 4.6x Net Debt to LTM adj. EBITDA in 2Q, per our calculations); we look out for liability management and/or return of capital announcements during 2024. On the 3Q23 earnings call, management cited 1-1.5x as an optimal longer-term leverage profile. Our price target of R\$17 represents +2% upside, and implies 21x 2025E P/E. We look for greater operating and capital structure visibility (aiding conviction in earnings estimates / trading multiple optionality) in order to revisit our Equal-weight rating.

## Risk Reward - Natura & Co Hold<sub>Risk Reward – Natura & Co Holding SA (NTCO3.SA)</sub>

Global Beauty Exposure; Integration & Macro Uncertainty Keeps Us On Sidelines

## R\$17.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from a BRL-denominated DCF that assumes a 13.9% cost of capital, 5.5% terminal growth rate, and 6.4% terminal adjusted EBIT margins. PT implies 6.8x EV/EBITDA and 20.7x P/E 2025e.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Natura's early-2020 Avon acquisition
created a beauty businesses with a global presence. However, the combination of Avon turnaround headwinds and external
macro/cost pressures have limited margin <sup>fl</sup>ow-through.
▪ We see limited visibility on margins, and await further clarity from new leadership around corporate expense streamlining,
Avon/Natura LatAm integration, and Avon International footprint optimiziation, among other areas.
▪ While trading multiples have de-rated
from highs, we are EW-rated awaiting
greater clarity on the path to a positive
operating in ection.

## BULL CASE

## Global Tailwinds Re-emerge

R\$25.00

Natura turns around the Avon business and accelerates Natura brand expansion into new markets, including China. Bull Case implies revenue grows at a +6% CAGR over 2023-2026e, including +5% for Natura &Co LatAm, +9% for Avon International. EBITDA grows at an +15% CAGR. Bull Case value is based on 7.6x EBITDA on our 2025e Bull Case estimates; implies \~22x Bull Case P/E 2025e.

## BASE CASE

## R\$17.00

## Integration Opportunity, but Re-Investment Needs

Natura executes in its core business lines, while Avon deal synergies are balanced by re-investment needs and macro/external expense pressures. Base Case implies revenue grows at a +4% CAGR over 2023- 2026e, including +3% for Natura &Co LatAm, +6% for Avon International. EBITDA grows at a +11% CAGR. Base case value is DCF-based and implies 6.8x EV/EBITDA and 21x P/E 2025e on our base case estimates.

## BEAR CASE

R\$11.00

## Growth Headwinds Persist

The Avon turnaround faces headwinds, driving greater re-investment needs, and international expansion slows. Bear Case implies revenue declines at a -2% CAGR over 2023-2026e, including -2% for Natura &Co LatAm, -2% for Avon International. EBITDA grows at a +2% CAGR. Bear Case value is based on \~5x EBITDA on our 2025e Bear Case estimates; implies \~19x Bear Case P/E 2025e.

## Petz (PETZ3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$4)

We highlight new store trends, same-store sales, and EBITDA margins as 3 items to watch for PETZ in '24. Awaiting stability in the sales/margin balance, we are EW-rated. For new store trends, we see Petz having slowed its opening pace from 50 stores in 2022 to our estimate of 33 stores in 2023E, but we see the year-end '23 store base still tracking a sizable \~50% above end-2021 levels and \~140% above end-2019. Disclosure on 4-wall EBITDA margins by cohort points to a potential margin tailwind as immature stores ramp up. However, following a year of decelerating sales and contracting EBITDA/net margins, we look for greater evidence in '24 that incremental new stores can show a similar rampup compared to earlier openings. For same-store sales, we see comps tracking to +4% for 2023E, below +9% in 2022 and +26% in 2021; for 2024, we forecast a rebound to +8% growth. We see tailwinds from new store maturation, a move past deflationary impacts, a potential sales shift back toward discretionary products, and an easy comparison base. While following the recent quarters' deceleration, including -0.5% comps in 3Q23, visibility on a positive inflection remains limited. For EBITDA margins, we see PETZ on track for - 70bps of y/y contraction in 2023E (to 7.0%, on a gross revenue, pre-IFRS16 basis), following -210bps of contraction in 2022. Pressures from digital mix, new unit maturity, and commercial aggressiveness more than offset SG&A efficiency efforts. Moving past peak headwinds, we forecast a partial improvement to 7.6% EBITDA margins (+60bps y/y) in 2024E. Including below-line items, we look for net margins to expand a more meaningful +130bps y/y (to 2.1%). Our price target goes to R\$4.0 (from R\$4.5), implying 24x 2024E P/E and 12x 2025E. With a structural growth opportunity, but limited earnings visibility amid an ongoing balance between sales- and margin-focused initiatives, we are EW-rated.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Petz is a leading pet products/services retailer in Brazil, operating \~250 stores in addition to veterinary centers and
eCommerce operations. The acquired
Zee.Dog business further expanded Petz presence into branded products and
international markets.
▪ We see room for the company's
superstore model to gain share versus
smaller/informal retailers, and Petz has been addressing this opportunity through
accelerated store growth and
complementary omni-channel operations. ▪ Balancing top line growth, pressure from in ation, eCommerce mix, acquisitions, and opening costs have weighed on margins; awaiting greater bottom-line ow through, we are EW-rated.

![](images/1dd69fa231e070c2f5bf3e83198c11cab7fe7c08f9b401674fdac29b0f31fd59.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## BULL CASE

## Pet Ecosystem Execution

R\$6.00

Petz executes on its ecosystem build-out, with store growth, eCommerce sales, and newer services monetization supporting top line expansion and margin ow-through. Revenue grows at a +17% CAGR between 2023-2026e, with +5% stores-only average SSS growth, +8% average area growth and +19% average eCommerce growth. Adjusted EBITDA (pre-IFRS16) grows at a +37% CAGR in the same period, with margin +540bps to 14.1% in 2026e. Bull Case fair value is based on 15x 2025e P/E on Bull Case estimates.

## BASE CASE

## R\$4.00

## Expansion Continues, with Growth Costs

Petz continues to grow its store count, capturing the superstore white space opportunity, while the impact of newer services initiatives remains modest, and eCommerce expansion requires continued growth investment. Revenue grows at a +13% CAGR between 2023-2026e, with +4% stores-only average SSS growth, +7% average area growth and +14% average eCommerce growth. Adjusted EBITDA (pre-IFRS16) grows at a +28% CAGR in the same period, with margin up +390bps to 12.3% in 2026e.

## BEAR CASE

R\$2.50

## Physical and Digital Pressure

Petz faces execution headwinds from a ramping store opening pace, driving sales/margin pressure and leading to a slower growth algorithm. Revenue grows at a +9% CAGR between 2023-2026e, with +3% stores-only average SSS growth, +5% average area growth and +11% average eCommerce growth. Adjusted EBITDA (pre-IFRS16) grows at a +15% CAGR in the same period, with margin up +140bps to 9.8% through 2026e. Bear Case fair value is based on 112x 2025e P/E on Bear Case estimates.

## Mobly S.A. (MBLY3.SA, Equal-weight, PT R\$2)

We highlight overall GMV, physical stores, and EBITDA margin trends as 3 items to watch for Mobly in '24. With a pressured operating backdrop largely reflected in the share price, we are EW-rated. For overall GMV, we see Mobly tracking to a -12% y/y decline in 2023E, on top of a -13% decline in 2022. This implies 2023E GMV +28% above 2019 (pre-pandemic) levels, a +6% CAGR. There has been a multiyear overhang from furniture demand pull-forward during peak pandemic periods, along with pressure from high interest rates. Our 2024 estimate calls for a modest rebound, at +13% y/y. For physical stores, omni-channel has been a pillar of Mobly's expansion and commercial strategy. While the physical stores '19-'23E GMV CAGR of +40% is above marketplace (+7%) and webshop (-5%), stores decelerated to just +4% growth for 2023E. We look for evidence that Mobly can resume megastore openings in '24 (versus -1 net closure for 2023), in order to re-build conviction around this potential growth driver. For EBITDA margin trends, we look for 2023E EBITDA margins (pre-IFRS16) of -10.9%, similar to - 11.3% in 2022, as expense control efforts balance de-leverage on the lower sales base. Into 2024E, we look for an improvement to -8.0% margins, while our model incorporates Mobly reaching positive EBITDA only in 2026E, and positive net income only in 2027E. Our price target goes to R\$2.0 (from R\$2.25), as we update estimates for Mobly following 3Q results, including 2024E revenue revised -12% and Adj. EBITDA margins (post-IFRS16) revised -300bps (to -0.4%). Our DCF reflects updated operating estimates, a roll-forward to year-end 2024, and an increased WACC (to 15.5%, from 14.8%, reflecting a continued backdrop of higher interest rates in Brazil); our new R\$2.0 PT is largely in-line with the current trading price, and implies 0.3x our 2024E gross profit. Balancing pressured top line trends and limited visibility on profitability, with recent-quarters' expense control focus and a potential improvement in furniture cyclical trends, we stay EW-rated.

## Risk Reward - Mobly S.<sub>Risk Reward – Mobly S.A. (MBLY3.SA)</sub>

Brazil Home & Living Omni-Channel Growth, but a Show-Me on Execution

## R\$2.00PRICE TARGET

Our end-2024 price target is derived from a BRL-denominated DCF, with a 15.5% WACC, 6.0% terminal growth rate, and 5.7% terminal adjusted EBIT margins. PT implies 0.3x EV/Gross Pro t 2025.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Mobly is a Brazil-based, online and omnichannel furniture retailer, with a broad product offering across home-related verticals.

▪ We see tailwinds for Mobly from the online shift and from store growth, but persistent furniture category headwinds weigh on expansion prospects, and on Mobly's ability to re-invest around newer initiatives.

▪ Balancing a supportive secular backdrop, headwinds including a post-pandemic category demand overhang, marketplace competition, and digital expansion costs keep us EW-rated.

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Self-help: Special Situation:

Positive Ne<sub>g</sub>ative Positive

## BULL CASE

R\$4.00

## Regaining Omni Furniture Leadership

A cyclical furniture upswing drives an online acceleration, bringing earlier scale gains, while productive store growth expands Mobly's TAM. GMV grows at a +25% CAGR between 2023 and 2026E, with Webshop at +14%, marketplace at +13% and stores at +43%; physical reaches 50% of the total. Adjusted EBITDA margin reaches 12.7%. Bull case fair value based on 0.8x our 2025E bull case Gross Pro t estimates.

## BASE CASE

## R\$2.00

## Secular Growth, Competitive Backdrop

Mobly's online furniture operations bene t from channel migration and market consolidation, while competitive headwinds and category pressure balance company initiatives. Mobly's potential strategic value is unlocked. GMV grows at an +22% CAGR between 2023 and 2026E, with Webshop at +13%, marketplace at +13% and stores at +37%; physical reaches 47% of the total. Adjusted EBITDA margin expands +1,260bps in the period, reaching 9.9%.

## BEAR CASE

## R\$1.00

## Competition Pressures The Model

Online growth decelerates and pro tability lags amid increasing competitive intensity. GMV grows at a +15% CAGR between 2023 and 2026E, with Webshop at +10%, marketplace at +9% and stores at +25%; physical reaches 43% of the total. Adjusted EBITDA margin reaches -3.2%. Bear case fair value based on 0.3x our 2025E bear case Gross Pro t estimates.

Despegar Consensus Revenue Revision

## Despegar.com Corp (DESP.N, Overweight, PT US\$13)

We highlight gross bookings, EBITDA margins, and FCF conversion as 3 items to watch for Despegar in '24. With secular online travel gains and profitable growth momentum not appearing priced in, we are OW-rated. For gross bookings, we see Despegar tracking to +30% y/y (to US\$5.3 billion) for 2023E, marking a +12% increase versus 2019 / prepandemic levels (including the impact of acquisitions). We see tailwinds into 2024, with room for a continued demand recovery (particularly for international travel), an industry shift toward online bookings, and pressure at Brazil local competitors (including a judicial recovery filing for 123Milhas). We forecast gross bookings +15% y/y for 2024E, which we believe could ultimately prove conservative. For EBITDA margins, in December 2023 Despegar announced raised 2023 financial guidance, calling for revenues of US\$690- 700m (from US\$670-700 million prior) and EBITDA of US\$105-110 million (from US\$90- 100 million) prior. This implies EBITDA margins of 15.5% at the midpoint, up from 7.6% in 2022 and following negative years in 2020/2021. Targets from the company's June 2022 investor day included EBITDA increasing 5x versus 2019 levels (implying \~US\$130 million) once the travel industry fully recovers, which we estimate as 2024. With Despegar already achieving US\$105-110 million for 2023E, we believe our current 2024 estimate of US\$132 million / 17.5% margin (not yet reflecting the guidance announcement) could prove conservative. For FCF conversion, management has guided to mid-60%'s of EBITDA long-term. We see support from a favorable working capital cycle in online travel, but note that due to various effects (incl. pandemic impacts, M&A), NWC changes have been closer to neutral in recent years. While we believe shares already screen attractive on an EBITDA basis (6.5x 2024E, including factoring expenses), execution toward FCF conversion targets could drive greater visibility on cash flow-based valuation support as well. Our price target of US\$13 represents +38% upside; coupling discounted valuation with operating momentum, we are Overweight-rated.

## US\$13.00PRICE TARGET

Our end-2024 price target is derived from a discounted cash ow analysis with a 16.3% cost of capital, 5.5% terminal growth rate, and 21.1% terminal adj. EBITDA margins. PT implies 6.9x 2025 EBITDA (including factoring expenses) and 18x 2024 P/E, which compares to the post-IPO average of 9x forward EBITDA (outside of pandemic periods).

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Despegar is the leading online travel agency (OTA) in Latin America, with \~US\$4bn in 2022 gross bookings. We see cyclical, secular, and company-speci c drivers supporting revenue growth. ▪ With mix drivers supporting take rates, and with our benchmarking analyses showing areas of scale leverage, we see a credible path for EBITDA to increase by 5x versus 2019 levels once the travel industry fully recovers (est. 2024). ▪ DESP shares trade at a discount versus historical levels and global peers, creating downside protection, while execution around newer initiatives (such as Fintech) supports upside optionality.

![](images/368b058e2b20a621b2d654bb5de8bc2ded75bb9a607981d629f1a00f63205173.jpg)

## BULL CASE

## Taking Flight

## US\$17.00 BASE CASE

A macro rebound supports LatAm online travel demand, with Despegar's share gains accelerating on the back of new business initiatives (including Fintech). Revenue grows at a +14% CAGR between 2023- 2026e, with a +10% gross bookings CAGR and 14.5% 2026e take rates. Adjusted EBITDA grows at a +42% CAGR in the same period, with margins up +1,380bps to 28.7% in 2025e. Bull Case fair value is based on 6.2x 2024e EBITDA on Bull Case estimates.

## Traveling LatAm

Despegar bene ts from a LatAm travel recovery, capturing share in the online fichannel, with scale gains enabling operating leverage. Revenue grows at a +9% CAGR between 2023-2026e, with a +10% gross bookings CAGR and 12.6% 2026e take rates. Adjusted EBITDA grows at a +24% CAGR in the same period, with margins up +640bps to 20.4% in 2026e.

## US\$13.00 BEAR CASE

US\$4.50

## Staying Grounded

Macro headwinds weigh on the LatAm travel recovery, and Despegar's investment needs increase in order to drive growth. Acquired operations (including Fintech) weigh on margins. Revenue grows at a +3% CAGR between 2023-2026e, with a +10% gross bookings CAGR and 10.5% 2026e take rates. Adjusted EBITDA grows at a +1% CAGR in the same period, with margins contracting -80bps to 12.3% in 2026e. Bear Case fair value is based on 4.3x 2025e EBITDA on Bear Case estimates.

# Walmart de Mexico y Centroamerica (WALMEX.MX, Overweight, PT M\$78)

We highlight Mexico comps, EBITDA margins, and COFECE updates as 3 items to watch for Walmex in '24. With shares at a discount to history, for earnings consistency which we expect to persist, we are OW-rated. For Mexico comps, we forecast a deceleration from +8.1% for 2023E to +7.0% for 2024E. We see a headwind from disinflation in food prices, but see support from consumer conditions in Mexico. On the back of price investments and digital initiatives, we expect Walmex to drive a continued outperformance gap versus the ANTAD industry. Within digital operations, we forecast online sales +20% y/y for 2024E, reaching 6.1% of Walmex's total in Mexico; this implies eCommerce contributes \~110bps to our comp growth forecast. For EBITDA margins, we forecast Walmex near-flat y/y at 11.0% in Mexico, and with \~10bps of y/y expansion to 10.8% in 2024E when including Central America operations. Within digital operations, Walmex has a track record of efficiency gains and unit economics rationality (i.e., charging delivery fees), supporting our conviction that online penetration can increase without degrading consolidated EBITDA margins. We further see Walmex better-positioned than smaller-scale peers to deal with rising minimum wages. While if policies that further drive higher expenses (such as a shortened work week) get enacted, we believe amid a lowmargin industry that food retailers would likely respond by passing through higher pricing. For COFECE updates, commentary in our November 2023 management meetings pointed to May 2024 as the timeline for a panel decision regarding an investigation around alleged relative monopolistic practices in the retail & consumer goods segment by Walmex. We believe a favorable decision would remove an overhang in the shares, while our analysis of prior COFECE cases in our WMX versus FMX deep-dive gives us comfort that an outsized remedy (a statutory 8% of revenue representing \~US\$3 billion) can be viewed as more of an outsized tail risk (versus a base or even bear case scenario). Our price target of M\$78 represents +11% upside, and we see a \~3% dividend yield for 2024E. Coupling steady expected growth with shares at 21x 2024E P/E (below the \~23x historical average), we are OW-rated.

Best-In-Class Mexico Food Retailer, with Omni and New Initiatives Upside

## M\$78.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from a MXN-denominated DCF that assumes an 12.2% cost of capital, 6.0% terminal growth rate, and 8.7% terminal adj. EBIT margins. PT implies \~21x 2024e P/E.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Walmex is a leading grocery and general merchandise retailer, with >50% share of the formal self-service grocery market in Mexico. ROIC is industry-leading, at >20% versus the Mexico peer average of 10%.
▪ With its omni-channel capabilities
(furthered by Walmart links) and with longstanding online grocery experience, we
believe Walmex's eCommerce business
can grow from less than 2% of sales in 2019 to \~6% in 2024, with growth and
pro <sub>fi</sub>tability in balance.
▪ We believe new initiatives such as Cashi ( ntech) and BAIT (connectivity) can further increase loyalty within the Walmex
ecosystem, while Walmart Connect
(advertising) has potential to become a
monetization driver.

## BULL CASE

## Pulling Away from Peers

M\$95.00

Positive cycle of growth and ROIC expansion, with omni-channel execution and a growing ads contribution. Bull Case implies revenue grows at a 12% CAGR over 2022-2025e (12% Mexico, 12% Central Am.), with comps +9.0% on average in constant FX (9.1% Mexico, 8.6% CA), selling area at a +3.6% CAGR (3.5% Mexico, 5.2% CA) and eCommerce penetration reaching \~7% in 2025e. EBITDA margin expands +40bps, reaching 11.1% in 2025e. Bull Case value is based on \~23x P/E on our 2025e Bull Case estimates.

## BASE CASE

## M\$78.00

## Best-In-Class Operator; Omni-Channel Upside

Walmex grows steadily with increasing omni-channel share, while continuing to improve returns. Base Case implies revenue grows at a 9% CAGR over 2022-2025e (9% Mexico, 9% Central Am.), with comps growing 7.0% on average in constant FX (7.1% Mexico, 6.7% CA), selling area increasing at a 1.9% CAGR (1.6% Mexico, 3.7% CA) and eCommerce penetration reaching \~7% in 2025e. EBITDA margin slightly expands +20bps to 10.9% in 2025e.

## BEAR CASE

M\$51.00

## Competition Pressures Returns

Growth slows, from eCommerce competition and macro headwinds. Bear Case implies revenue grows at a 6% CAGR over 2022-2025e (6% Mexico, 7% Central Am.), with comps 5.1% on average in constant FX (5.1% Mexico, 4.7% CA); and eCommerce penetration is \~6.5% in 2025e. EBITDA margin contracts -50bps, reaching 10.2% in 2025e. Unfavorable resolution of monopolistic practices investigation pressures multiple. Bear Case value is based on 16.5x P/E on our 2025e Bear Case estimates.

## El Puerto de Liverpool (LIVEPOLC1.MX, Equal-weight, PT M \$119)

We highlight same-store sales, EBITDA margins, and newer initiatives as 3 items to watch for Liverpool in '24. With upward revisions driving down trading multiples and supporting more balanced risk-reward, we upgrade to EW. For same-store sales, we look for Liverpool to deliver +5.8% comps in ‘24E, marking general stability but a slight deceleration from the +8.1% tracking for 2023E; by banner, we look for Liverpool to continue above Suburbia. We see Liverpool’s overall retail sales tracking +37% above 2019 (pre-pandemic) levels for 2023E, disproving our earlier concerns around more pressured trends in ’23 amid a difficult comparison base. For EBITDA margins, we forecast -110bps of y/y contraction to 16.1% in ‘23E (incl. IFRS-16), following strong margins in the \~17% level for ’22 and ‘23E. Amid continued digital competition/costs and a decelerating top line trend, we look for margins to revert more in-line with 2019 levels. Liverpool’s credit business has been a source of strength in recent years, with below-trend NPLs, and part of our forecast margin contraction is on an increase in provisions, normalizing closer to the historical rate. For newer initiatives, Liverpool has in place an early-stage electric partnership for electric car sales with China-based BYD (covered by Tim Hsiao); commentary on initial sales was tepid in 3Q23, while this opens up a large addressable market. Growth in eCommerce marketplace and newer financial products were additionally in-focus at the March 2023 Investor Day, while we await signs of traction in these areas. Our rating moves to Equal-weight (from Underweight), as despite fundamental department store caution and the eCommerce threat, Liverpool has seen upward estimate revisions and the shares trade at an undemanding 8x 2024E P/E multiples. Crediting incremental stability to longer-term margins, our price target increases to M\$119 (from M\$108), implying 9x our 2024E EPS and representing +5% upside.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ El Puerto de Liverpool is a top player in Mexico department stores with >30% share, operating \~300 stores mainly under the banners Liverpool and Suburbia.
▪ We believe eCommerce headwinds to
department stores are global and will
extend to Mexico, with margin pressures from a combination of lower store sales, online/digital investments, and expanding competition. While Liverpool's post
pandemic recovery helps disprove these concerns for the near-term.
▪ At current levels, Liverpool trades at an undemanding multiple, supporting our EW rating.

![](images/689afad26c84fe4f99314fb3c3f5d2d2d975f7e199b619a8bb0291df19ee2143.jpg)
