## BULL CASE

## Bucking the Trend

M\$142.00

Liverpool sees a persistent traf c recovery, supporting the ability to fund digital fiinvestments with store expense leverage. Bull Case implies revenue grows at a +11% CAGR over 2023-2026e (10% Liverpool, 13% Suburbia), with comps growing 7% on average (7% Liverpool, 6% Suburbia), and selling area increasing at a 7% CAGR over the period (6% Liverpool, 9% Suburbia). EBITDA margin contracts -80bps, reaching 16.6% in 2025e. Bull Case value is based on 8.0x P/E on our 2025e Bull Case estimates.

## BASE CASE

## M\$119.00

## Muted Department Store Growth

Liverpool's business sees muted growth trends as a Mexico department store pureplay. Base Case implies revenue grows at an +8% CAGR over 2023-2026e (+7% Liverpool, +11% Suburbia), with comps growth at +6% on average (+6% Liverpool, +5% Suburbia), and selling area increasing at a +4% CAGR over the period (+3% Liverpool, +9% Suburbia). EBITDA margin contracts -180bps to 15.4% in 2026e.

## BEAR CASE

M\$73.00

## Industry Conditions Deteriorate

Liverpool faces renewed macro and store traf c headwinds, along with rising digitalization costs. Bear Case implies revenue grows at a +4% CAGR over 2023- 2026e (+4% Liverpool, +3% Suburbia), with comps +3% on average (+3% Liverpool, +2% Suburbia), while selling area grows at a +2% CAGR over the period (+2% Liverpool, +2% Suburbia). EBITDA margin contracts -350bps, reaching 13.1% in 2026e. Bear Case value is based on 7.0x P/E on our 2025e Bear Case estimates.

## Falabella S.A. (FALABELLA.SN, Underweight, PT Ch\$1,740)

We highlight Chile comps, EBITDA margins, and asset monetization as 3 items to watch for Falabella in '24. With limited valuation support even when incorporating a sales/margin recovery in the year ahead, we downgrade to UW (from EW). For Chile comps, we forecast a +3.8% increase in 2024E, marking a positive inflection following an - 11.5% decline in 2023E and -7.1% in 2022 (upon lapping consumer stimulus measures). For Falabella's Chile retail sales overall, our estimates imply a modest +5% 2019-2024E CAGR. While lower interest rates could drive a consumption tailwind, our economists see conditions remaining tight in the year ahead; further, we believe the pull-forward of spending amid pension fund withdrawal stimulus in 2020/2021 could weigh on the magnitude of a top line recovery. Our Chile forecasts for Falabella include eCommerce GMV +14% y/y, with penetration increasing \~200bps to 30%. For EBITDA margins, Falabella contracted from a peak of 12.5% (ex-IFRS16) in 2021, to 6.2% in 2022, and we see the business tracking for a further contraction at 5.0% for 2023E. Operating de-leverage amid lower sales growth and continued digitalization investments has more than outweighed the impact of efficiency initiatives. Our estimates imply EBITDA margins +140bps y/y (to 6.4%) for 2024, but we see limited support for a more meaningful recovery. For asset monetization, along with 3Q earnings management laid out a noncore asset monetization program (mainly real estate) of between US\$800 million and US \$1 billion over the next 12 to 15 months; since that time, a potential deal for real estate assets in Peru was announced, while this would reflect a related party transaction with subsidiary Mallplaza (covered by Alejandra Obregon). We note that Falabella ended 3Q23 with 8.2x Net Debt / LTM EBITDA, and the asset monetization targets equate to 1.6x to 2.0x of Falabella's LTM EBITDA. Our stock rating goes to Underweight (from Equalweight), with a negative risk-reward skew following share price appreciation but negative earnings revision trends in 2023; we believe the EBITDA/NI recovery implied in Street estimates is too favorable. We see premium trading multiples (31x 2024E P/E and 18x 2025E) even when incorporating a sales/margin rebound for the year ahead. Our price target is maintained at Ch\$1,740 and implies 24x '24E EPS and 14x '25E; our target reflects updated operating estimates post-3Q earnings ('24E revenue revised -2% / EBITDA +5%), and a roll-forward to an end-2024 basis. We see -22% PT downside, with a 0.3:1.0 bullbear skew.

## Risk Reward - Falabella S.A. <sub>Risk Reward – Falabella S.A. (FALABELLA.SN)</sub>

Margin Reset Drives a Negative Risk-Reward Skew

## Ch\$1,740.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from the average of a DCF analysis (\~Ch\$1,780) and a Sum of the Parts valuation (\~Ch\$1,700). Our DCF assumes an 9.7% cost of capital, 4.2% terminal growth rate, and 6.5% terminal adjusted EBIT margins for the whole company. In our SOTP, we value Chile and Peru at 7.0x and 5.5x FY1 EV/EBITDA, respectively, while the Mallplaza stake is valued based on Alejandra Obregon's PT.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Falabella is a Chile-based multi-format and multi-country retailer. While department stores t within our cautious global framework, we are more constructive on home improvement and see stability in grocery and mall operations. ▪ The combination of Chile macro headwinds and eCommerce investments have pressured margins, more than offsetting the impact of ef ficiency initiatives; while lower EBITDA has driven up Falabella's <sub>fi</sub>nancial leverage. ▪ Even when incorporating improving sales/margin trends as the Chile macro cycle turns, we see premium P/E multiples, which in our view limits upside potential for the stock.

## BULL CASE

## Ch\$2,550.00

## Macro and Omni-Channel Upside

Omni-channel execution supports sales/margins, with FAL more effectively capturing scale bene ts across its countries and formats of operation; marketplace and digital nancial services gain traction. Bull Case implies net revenue grows at a 6% CAGR over 2023E-2026E, with Chile growing at a 7% CAGR and Peru at 6% (CLP). EBITDA margin expands +470bps from 2023E levels, reaching 12.0% in 2026E. Bull Case is based on 13x P/E on our Bull Case 2025E estimates.

## BASE CASE

## Ch\$1,740.00

## Margin Rebound Faces Pressure

FAL continues its shift to a digital platform approach, supporting the long-term top line outlook, but macro and transformation headwinds weigh on the pace of the margin recovery. Base Case implies net revenue grows at a 5% CAGR over 2023E-2026E, with Chile growing at a 5% CAGR and Peru at an 6% CAGR (CLP). EBITDA margin expands +310bps above 2023 levels, at 9.9% in 2026E.

## BEAR CASE

## Ch\$880.00

## Macro Takes a Toll

Falabella faces increasing operating pressure on the combination of abating Chile stimulus payments and higher digital expenses, weighing on leverage metrics. Bear Case implies net revenue grows at a 4% CAGR over 2023E-2026E, with Chile at +3% and Peru at +4% (CLP). EBITDA margin expands +260bps from 2023E levels, reaching 9.3% in 2026E. Bear Case is based on 9.3x P/E on our Bear Case 2025E estimates.

Cencosud Historical 12m Forward P/E

## Cencosud (CENCOSUD.SN, Equal-weight, PT Ch\$1,970)

We highlight Chile comps, US/Brazil expansion, and EBITDA margins as 3 items to watch for Cencosud in '24. Cencosud is our preferred name in Chile retail, while with greater upside elsewhere in our food retail coverage we are EW-rated. For Chile comps, we see Cencosud tracking to +1.3% y/y growth for 2023E, which we see as a notable affirmation of food retail stability amid a difficult comparison base (lapping +20.5% growth in 2021 and +13.4% in 2022). Into 2024E, we forecast a modest pickup to +3.2% comps, with supermarkets, home improvement, and department stores each around the \~3% level. Versus 2023 levels, we look for decelerating trends in food retail, more than offset by a return to slight growth in Cencosud's more discretionary segments (home improvement and department stores) amid a depressed comparison base. For US/Brazil expansion, Cencosud's US-based Fresh Market (TFM) operation has averaged just 1.1% comps in the 5 quarters since 3Q22 (where the contribution was initially disclosed). These operations accounted for 12% of Cencosud's revenue in 2023E, and we estimate a similar proportion for 2024E, while changes in US consumer conditions (with TFM exposed to the premium grocery segment) could be a swing factor. Within Brazil, including acquired Giga Atacadao operations we see 11% revenue exposure to Brazil for 2023E, increasing to 12% for 2024E. We are constructive on cash & carry industry trends, but see scale advantages more-so for national operators. For EBITDA margins, we forecast flat y/y trends at 9.0% (ex-IFRS16) for 2024E. We see Cencosud tracking to a -80bp y/y contraction in 2023E (to 9.0%), while this reversion is more modest than we had initially feared, with the company settling in well above the pre-pandemic \~7.0% level. We note that Cencosud has exposure to Argentina, while our post-3Q model update incorporated the impact of the December 2023 Argentine peso devaluation. Our price target of Ch\$1,970 represents +20% upside, and we see discounted valuation with the shares at \~8x 2024E and 2025E earnings on our estimates; while absent a clearer catalyst path for value unlock, we stay EW-rated.

## Risk Reward - Cencosud SA <sub>Risk Reward – Cencosud SA (CENCOSUD.SN)</sub>

Low-Growth Format Mix, but with Improving Execution

## Ch\$1,970.00PRICE TARGET

Our price target is derived from the average of a DCF analysis (\~Ch\$2,260) and a Sum of the Parts valuation (\~Ch\$1,680). Our DCF assumes a 11.6% cost of capital, 4.0% terminal growth rate, and 6.3% terminal adjusted EBIT margins. Our SOTP includes supermarkets at 3x FY1 EV/EBITDA, home improvement at 7.5x, and <sup>fi</sup>nancial services at 1.6x.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Cencosud is a scaled player in the supermarkets business in LatAm (primarily Chile), with additional operations in home improvement, department stores, malls, and <sup>fi</sup>nancial services. ▪ The company's expansion into the US helps diversify operational exposure, but we see limited synergies between The Fresh Market and core Cencosud operations. ▪ Execution has shown signs of improvement in recent years, though we see top-down headwinds against a pressured retail sales backdrop in Chile.

![](images/e986385b539d289067de37cd2bad78b08c9baac629d64f5718e53bcb9b48f03e.jpg)

Risk Reward Themes Self-help: Positive Special Situation: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

Ch\$2,605.00

## Operational Improvement Sustains

CEN revenue/EBITDA growth continues even as Chile stimulus abates, enabling sustainable de-leverage. Bull Case implies Net Revenue grows at a +4% CAGR over 2023-2026e, and adjusted EBITDA increases at a +6% CAGR in the period, with margins expanding 60bps to 11.1%. Net Debt to EBITDA goes down to 0.9x by 2026e. Bull Case is based on 10.5x P/E on our Bull Case 2025e estimates.

BASE CASE

## Ch\$1,970.00

## Low Organic Top-Line Growth

CEN's topline growth deteriorates slightly, with outsized supermarket strength reverting and department stores facing pressure. Base Case assumes Net Revenue grows at a +3% CAGR over 2023-2026e, while adjusted EBITDA increases at a +2% CAGR in the period, with margins contracting -40bps, to 10.1% in 2026e. Net Debt to EBITDA reaches 1.3x by 2026e.

## BEAR CASE

Ch\$1,245.00

## Business Pressures Increase

A deterioration in business and macro conditions, including at TFM's US operations, drives an elevated leverage cycle. Bear Case implies Net Revenue grows at only a +2% CAGR over 2023-2026e, while adjusted EBITDA decreases at a -3% CAGR in the period, with margins contracting -130bps, to 8.9% in 2026e. Net Debt to EBITDA reaches 2.0x by 2026e. Bear Case is based on 9.0x P/E on our Bear Case 2025e estimates.

## Survey Demographics

Survey Methodology: Online survey among \~2,500 consumers aged 16–75 years old in Brazil and Mexico (\~1,250 per country). Total sample is representative of the countries’ general population in terms of age, gender and socioeconomic class.

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of al ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O or Over) - The stock's total return is expected to exceed the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months. Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months. Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
